   4: # Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md (Wire→Surface→Close→Re-fire pattern; this hook is Wire-stage for commit/push failure observability gap)
   5: # Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/layered-gates-architecture.md §6 Layer 3 (PostToolUse-class audit-trail role; async + fail-open)
   6: # Reference: TIER-3 audit-trail (3-agent SOTA wave 2026-04-30 Agent A F1 finding — T2/T3 currently blind to commit gate-deny, hook-block, lockfile contention)
   7: """PostToolUseFailure hook — capture git commit/push failure events to JSONL telemetry.
   8: 
   9: Closes the audit-action-loop §1 producer-consumer gap for the commit-failure
  10: surface. T2 (codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py) sometimes denies via permissionDecision;
  11: T3 (codex_postcommit_review.py) only fires on success. Without this hook,
  12: commit-deny events are invisible to downstream surfaces.
  13: 
  14: Discipline:
  15: - Fail-open: any error → exit 0 with no JSONL write (per Layer 3 audit-trail role)
  16: - Async: settings.json registers with `async: true`, timeout 10s
  17: - Bounded: ≤2KB per record (tool_name + error_truncated + ts + is_interrupt)
  18: - No model output: writes to JSONL only; doesn't surface to context
  19: """
  20: from __future__ import annotations
  21: 
  22: import json
  23: import sys
  24: from datetime import datetime, timezone
  25: from pathlib import Path
  26: 
  27: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
  28: from utils import log_swallow  # noqa: E402
  29: 
  30: _PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
  31: _STATE_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / ".claude" / "state"
  32: _JSONL = _STATE_DIR / "codex_failure_audit.jsonl"
  33: _MAX_ERROR_CHARS = 1500  # bound per record
  34: 
  35: 
  36: def _read_stdin_json() -> dict | None:
  37:     """Parse hook stdin per Anthropic SDK PostToolUseFailureHookInput schema."""
  38:     try:
  39:         raw = sys.stdin.read()
  40:         if not raw.strip():
  41:             return None
  42:         return json.loads(raw)
  43:     except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
  44:         log_swallow(exc, "codex_failure_audit.stdin_parse")
  45:         return None
  46: 
  47: 
  48: def main() -> int:
  49:     try:
  50:         payload = _read_stdin_json()
  51:         if not payload:
  52:             return 0  # fail-open: no stdin or parse failure
  53:         tool_name = payload.get("tool_name", "unknown")
  54:         tool_input = payload.get("tool_input", {})
  55:         error = str(payload.get("error", ""))[:_MAX_ERROR_CHARS]
  56:         is_interrupt = bool(payload.get("is_interrupt", False))
  57:         # Filter to git commit/push failures only — other tool failures are out of scope
  58:         cmd = str(tool_input.get("command", ""))
  59:         if "git commit" not in cmd and "git push" not in cmd:
  60:             return 0
  61:         record = {
  62:             "ts": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
  63:             "event": "post_tool_use_failure",
  64:             "tool_name": tool_name,
  65:             "command_prefix": cmd[:200],  # bound; no full command leak
  66:             "error_truncated": error,
  67:             "is_interrupt": is_interrupt,
  68:             "session_id": payload.get("session_id", "unknown"),
  69:         }
  70:         _STATE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  71:         with _JSONL.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
  72:             fh.write(json.dumps(record, separators=(",", ":")) + "\n")
  73:     except Exception as exc:
  74:         # Never block on telemetry failure (Layer 3 discipline)
  75:         # Observability via log_swallow (stderr + JSONL + counter)
  76:         log_swallow(exc, "codex_failure_audit.main")
  77:     return 0
  78: 
  79: 
  80: if __name__ == "__main__":
  81:     sys.exit(main())

 112: # `prefix-${X}-suffix` partial-string preservation. Stops at } / \r / \n to
 113: # bound matches to a single template token.
 114: _LITERAL_VAR_RE = re.compile(r"\$\{[^}\r\n]+\}")

 117: def _now_iso() -> str:
 118:     return datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()
 119: 
 120: 
 121: def _normalize(file_path: str) -> str:
 122:     if not file_path:
 123:         return ""
 124:     norm = file_path.replace("\\", "/")
 125:     if len(norm) >= 2 and norm[1] == ":":
 126:         norm = norm[:2].lower() + norm[2:]
 127:     return norm

 129: 
 130: def _resolve_event_path(raw_fp: str, event_cwd: str) -> str:
 131:     """Resolve raw file_path against event.cwd if relative; normalize POSIX+drive."""
 132:     if not raw_fp:
 133:         return ""
 134:     p = Path(raw_fp)
 135:     if not p.is_absolute() and event_cwd:
 136:         try:
 137:             p = (Path(event_cwd) / raw_fp).resolve(strict=False)
 138:         except (OSError, ValueError):
 139:             pass
 140:     return _normalize(str(p))
 240:             for k, v in env_headers.items():
 241:                 for f in _scan_value_for_literal(
 242:                     v, f"{name}.transport.env_http_headers[{k}]",
 243:                 ):
 244:                     findings.append({"server": name, "header": k, **f})
 245:     return findings

 248: def _resolve_inner_timeout() -> float:
 249:     """E3 timeout headroom: clamp to leave >=2s under outer hook timeout."""
 250:     raw = os.environ.get("CODEX_MCP_HEALTHCHECK_TIMEOUT_SEC")

 253:     except (TypeError, ValueError):
 254:         t = _DEFAULT_INNER_TIMEOUT_SEC
 255:     upper = max(1.0, _OUTER_HOOK_TIMEOUT_SEC - _MIN_HEADROOM_SEC)
 256:     return max(1.0, min(t, upper))

  70:     """Return (source_jsonl_path, row) tuples from all configured launch logs."""

  93: def _pid_alive(pid: int) -> bool:
  94:     """Cross-platform: check if a PID is currently alive."""
  95:     try:
  96:         if _IS_WINDOWS:
  97:             import ctypes  # noqa: PLC0415
  98: 
  99:             kernel32 = ctypes.windll.kernel32
 100:             handle = kernel32.OpenProcess(
 101:                 _PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION,
 102:                 False,
 103:                 pid,
 104:             )
 105:             if not handle:
 106:                 return False
 107:             try:
 108:                 return True
 109:             finally:
 110:                 kernel32.CloseHandle(handle)
 111:         os.kill(pid, 0)
 112:         return True
 113:     except (OSError, ProcessLookupError):
 114:         return False

 117: def _kill_pid(pid: int) -> bool:
 118:     """Cross-platform: force-kill a PID and its descendants."""
 119:     try:
 120:         if _IS_WINDOWS:
 121:             result = run_guard_subprocess(  # noqa: S603
 122:                 ["taskkill", "/T", "/F", "/PID", str(pid)],
 123:                 capture_output=True,
 124:                 text=True,
 125:                 timeout=10,
 126:                 check=False,
 127:             )
 128:             if result.returncode in (0, 128):
 129:                 return True
 130:             print(
 131:                 f"codex_stuck_detector: taskkill({pid}) exit={result.returncode} "
 132:                 f"stderr={result.stderr.strip()!r}",
 133:                 file=sys.stderr,
 134:             )
 135:             return False
 136:         os.kill(pid, signal.SIGTERM)
 137:         time.sleep(0.5)
 138:         if _pid_alive(pid):
 139:             os.kill(pid, _SIGKILL)
 140:         return True
 141:     except (OSError, ProcessLookupError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired) as e:
 142:         print(f"codex_stuck_detector: kill({pid}) failed: {e!r}", file=sys.stderr)
 143:         return False

 165: def _clean_orphan_streams() -> int:
 166:     """Unlink zero-byte codex_review_HEAD_*.stream.jsonl files older than threshold."""
 167:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_STREAM_JANITOR_DISABLE") == "1":
 168:         return 0
 169:     age_threshold_min = int(os.environ.get("CODEX_STREAM_JANITOR_AGE_MIN", "30"))
 170:     age_threshold_sec = age_threshold_min * 60.0
 171:     now = time.time()
 172:     cleaned = 0
 173:     try:
 174:         for stream_path in _STATE_DIR.glob("codex_review_HEAD_*.stream.jsonl"):
 175:             try:
 176:                 st = stream_path.stat()
 177:             except OSError:
 178:                 continue
 179:             if st.st_size != 0:
 180:                 continue
 181:             if (now - st.st_mtime) < age_threshold_sec:
 182:                 continue
 183:             try:
 184:                 stream_path.unlink()
 185:                 cleaned += 1
 186:                 print(
 187:                     f"codex_stuck_detector: janitor unlinked orphan stream {stream_path.name} "

 190:                 )
 191:             except OSError as e:
 192:                 print(
 193:                     f"codex_stuck_detector: janitor unlink failed for {stream_path.name}: {e!r}",

 195:                 )
 196:     except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
 197:         print(f"codex_stuck_detector: janitor scan failed: {e!r}", file=sys.stderr)
 198:     return cleaned

 201: def main() -> int:
 202:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_STUCK_DETECTOR_DISABLE") == "1":
 203:         return 0
 204: 
 205:     threshold_min = int(os.environ.get("CODEX_STUCK_THRESHOLD_MIN", "10"))
 206:     min_bytes = int(os.environ.get("CODEX_STUCK_MIN_OUTPUT_BYTES", "500"))
 207: 
 208:     _clean_orphan_streams()
 209: 
 210:     with contextlib.suppress(Exception):
 211:         sys.stdin.read()
 212: 
 213:     rows = _read_jsonl_rows()
 214:     if not rows:
 215:         return 0
 216: 
 217:     now = time.time()
 218:     latest = _latest_launch_by_pid(rows)
 219:     killed_count = 0
 220: 
 221:     for pid, (src, row) in latest.items():
 222:         if row.get("status") != "launched":
 223:             continue
 224: 
 225:         ts_epoch = _parse_iso_ts(row.get("ts", ""))
 226:         if ts_epoch == 0:
 227:             continue
 228:         age_min = (now - ts_epoch) / 60.0
 229:         if age_min < threshold_min:
 230:             continue
 231: 
 232:         outfile_path = row.get("outfile")
 233:         if not outfile_path:
 234:             continue
 235:         outfile = Path(outfile_path)
 236:         size = outfile.stat().st_size if outfile.exists() else 0
 237: 
 238:         if size >= min_bytes:
 239:             continue
 240: 
 241:         if not _pid_alive(pid):
 242:             continue
 243: 
 244:         killed = _kill_pid(pid)
 245:         kill_record = {
 246:             "sha": row.get("sha"),
 247:             "pid": pid,
 248:             "outfile": outfile_path,
 249:             "status": "stuck_killed" if killed else "stuck_kill_failed",
 250:             "age_min": round(age_min, 1),
 251:             # Fire 3.b instrumentation per Fire 3 codex T1 F-DUR-002 deferred scope
 252:             # (commit f995825). Sister field to `commit_duration_ms` on
 253:             # codex_postcommit/prepush_review.py launch rows; enables paired-latency
 254:             # analysis (commit-detection-to-codex-spawn vs codex-spawn-to-stuck-kill).
 255:             "launch_age_ms": int(age_min * 60_000),
 256:             "output_bytes": size,
 257:             "threshold_min": threshold_min,
 258:             "ts": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()),
 259:         }
 260:         try:
 261:             with src.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
 262:                 f.write(json.dumps(kill_record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
 263:         except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
 264:             print(
 265:                 f"codex_stuck_detector: jsonl write failed for {src}: {e!r}",
 266:                 file=sys.stderr,
 267:             )
 268:         if killed:
 269:             killed_count += 1
 270:             print(
 271:                 f"codex_stuck_detector: killed stuck pid={pid} age={age_min:.1f}min output={size}B",
 272:                 file=sys.stderr,
 273:             )
 274: 
 275:     # Sibling thread-bridge integration: best-effort import, skip if not ported yet.
 276:     try:
 277:         sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
 278:         from codex_review_thread_bridge import bridge_unresolved_rows  # type: ignore[import-not-found]
 279: 
 280:         for log_path in _LAUNCH_LOGS:
 281:             try:
 282:                 bridge_unresolved_rows(log_path, max_rows=10)
 283:             except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
 284:                 print(
 285:                     f"codex_stuck_detector: bridge({log_path.name}) failed: {e!r}",
 286:                     file=sys.stderr,
 287:                 )
 288:     except ImportError:
 289:         # Not ported into sss yet — silent skip (port-time deferred dep).
 290:         pass
 291:     except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
 292:         print(f"codex_stuck_detector: bridge integration failed: {e!r}", file=sys.stderr)
 293: 
 294:     return 0
 295: 
 296: 
 297: if __name__ == "__main__":
 298:     sys.exit(main())

  77: def _parse_first_line_thread_id(stream_file: Path) -> str | None:
  78:     """Read first non-empty line of stream_file; return thread_id or None.
  79: 
  80:     Stream JSONL contract: line 1 of broker-tracked spawns is
  81:     {"type": "thread.started", "thread_id": "<uuid>"}. Ephemeral spawns
  82:     produce no stream file (or empty). On JSON parse error or wrong

  84:     """

 104: def resolve_thread_id(
 105:     launch_row: dict,
 106:     *,
 107:     timeout_s: float = _DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_S,
 108:     poll_interval_s: float = _DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_S,
 109: ) -> dict | None:
 110:     """Resolve broker thread_id from a launched-row's stream file.
 111: 
 112:     Inputs:
 113:         launch_row: dict with at least {sha, pid, stream_file, broker_tracked}
 114:         timeout_s: max wait for stream file to appear + first line to land
 115:         poll_interval_s: between polls
 116: 
 117:     Returns:
 118:         {"status": "thread_resolved", "sha", "pid", "stream_file",
 119:          "thread_id", "ts"} on success
 120:         None on: ephemeral row (broker_tracked=False), missing stream_file
 121:         path, no thread.started line within timeout, parse failure.

 123:     Fail-open: any exception -> None. Caller continues without bridging.
 124:     """
 125:     if not isinstance(launch_row, dict):

 127:     if launch_row.get("broker_tracked") is not True:
 128:         return None  # ephemeral spawns have no stream/thread
 129:     stream_file_str = launch_row.get("stream_file")
 130:     if not isinstance(stream_file_str, str) or not stream_file_str:
 131:         return None
 132: 
 133:     stream_file = Path(stream_file_str)
 134:     deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, timeout_s)
 135:     last_thread_id: str | None = None
 136:     while True:
 137:         if stream_file.exists():
 138:             last_thread_id = _parse_first_line_thread_id(stream_file)
 139:             if last_thread_id:
 140:                 break
 141:         if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
 142:             break
 143:         time.sleep(max(0.0, poll_interval_s))
 144: 
 145:     if not last_thread_id:
 146:         return None
 147: 
 148:     sha = launch_row.get("sha", "")
 149:     pid = launch_row.get("pid")
 150:     return {
 151:         "status": "thread_resolved",
 152:         "sha": sha if isinstance(sha, str) else "",
 153:         "pid": pid if isinstance(pid, int) else None,
 154:         "stream_file": stream_file_str,
 155:         "thread_id": last_thread_id,
 156:         "ts": _now_iso(),
 157:     }
 158: 
 159: 
 160: def _iter_jsonl_rows(path: Path):
 161:     """Yield parsed JSONL rows from path; skip malformed silently."""
 162:     try:
 163:         with path.open("r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
 164:             for line in f:
 165:                 line = line.strip()
 166:                 if not line:
 167:                     continue
 168:                 try:
 169:                     yield json.loads(line)
 170:                 except json.JSONDecodeError:
 171:                     continue
 172:     except OSError:
 173:         return
 174: 
 175: 
 176: def already_resolved_keys(path: Path) -> set[str]:
 177:     """Return set of stream_file paths that already have a thread_resolved row.
 178: 
 179:     Cycle-590 ship 17 fix-forward (commit 2bc254e9 -> fix-forward): switched
 180:     from (sha, pid) tuple key to stream_file path for stable per-launch
 181:     identity. Producers (codex_postcommit_review.py + codex_prepush_review.py)
 182:     now encode ts_unix-ms into stream_file naming, making each launch's path
 183:     unique even when same SHA reviewed multiple times (postcommit + prepush,
 184:     or amended/re-fired commit, or session-restart re-trigger).
 185: 
 186:     Convergence trail:
 187:     - codex GPT-5.5 review on Ship 16 (.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_2bc254e9.txt)
 188:       Finding 1 conf=0.82 high: "PID/SHA is not a stable launch identity"
 189:     - empirical probe: 3 stream_file collisions in 147 production rows
 190:       (595401d0, 77d6ef7d, 2bc254e9 — including ship-16's own commit which
 191:       had launch1 17:02:01 resolved to thread 019dc597-... and launch2
 192:       17:08:00 still unresolved sharing same pre-fix stream path)
 193:     - producer line :370/:374 with "wb" truncate-write race condition
 194: 
 195:     Used to skip re-bridging on subsequent iterations — idempotency.
 196:     """
 197:     seen: set[str] = set()
 198:     for row in _iter_jsonl_rows(path):
 199:         if row.get("status") != "thread_resolved":
 200:             continue
 201:         sf = row.get("stream_file", "")
 202:         if isinstance(sf, str) and sf:
 203:             seen.add(sf)
 204:     return seen
 205: 
 206: 
 207: def append_resolution(path: Path, resolution: dict) -> bool:
 208:     """Append a thread_resolved row to JSONL. Returns True on success.
 209: 
 210:     Atomic on POSIX <PIPE_BUF (4KB) and Windows O_APPEND <512B for our
 211:     typical 200-300 byte rows. Fail-open: log to stderr, return False.
 212:     """
 213:     try:
 214:         path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 215:         with path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
 216:             f.write(json.dumps(resolution, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
 217:         return True
 218:     except OSError as e:
 219:         print(
 220:             f"codex_review_thread_bridge: append_resolution failed: {e!r}",
 221:             file=__import__("sys").stderr,
 222:         )
 223:         return False
 224: 
 225: 
 226: def bridge_unresolved_rows(jsonl_path: Path, *, max_rows: int = 50) -> int:
 227:     """Walk a postcommit/prepush JSONL file and bridge unresolved threads.
 228: 
 229:     Iterates `status: launched` rows where broker_tracked=true and
 230:     (sha, pid) is not in already_resolved_keys. For each, calls
 231:     resolve_thread_id and appends the resolution if successful.
 232: 
 233:     Returns: count of newly-resolved rows.
 234: 
 235:     Bounded by max_rows to keep the Stop-hook responsive (each
 236:     resolve_thread_id call may sleep up to timeout_s polling for the
 237:     stream file). Default 50 covers typical session volume.
 238:     """
 239:     if not jsonl_path.exists():
 240:         return 0
 241:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_REVIEW_THREAD_BRIDGE_DISABLE") == "1":
 242:         return 0
 243: 
 244:     resolved_keys = already_resolved_keys(jsonl_path)
 245:     resolved_count = 0
 246: 
 247:     # Walk launched rows; skip rows whose stream_file already has a
 248:     # thread_resolved row (cycle-590 ship 17 fix-forward — was (sha, pid)
 249:     # tuple key, switched to stream_file path for stable per-launch
 250:     # identity. See already_resolved_keys() docstring for full rationale.)
 251:     for row in _iter_jsonl_rows(jsonl_path):
 252:         if resolved_count >= max_rows:
 253:             break
 254:         if row.get("status") != "launched":
 255:             continue
 256:         if row.get("broker_tracked") is not True:
 257:             continue
 258:         sha = row.get("sha", "")
 259:         if not isinstance(sha, str) or not sha:
 260:             continue
 261:         sf = row.get("stream_file", "")
 262:         if not isinstance(sf, str) or not sf:
 263:             # Pre-ship-17 launched rows lacked stream_file; cannot dedup
 264:             # safely. Skip — they'd otherwise re-resolve every fire and
 265:             # potentially write phantom thread_resolved rows.
 266:             continue
 267:         key = sf
 268:         if key in resolved_keys:
 269:             continue
 270:         # Use shorter timeout in batch mode — fast-path on healthy fires
 271:         # where stream file should already exist by the time the Stop-hook
 272:         # iterates (postcommit_review fires first, then turn completes,
 273:         # then Stop-hook picks up the trail).
 274:         resolution = resolve_thread_id(
 275:             row,
 276:             timeout_s=2.0,  # bounded — Stop-hook can't block long
 277:             poll_interval_s=0.25,
 278:         )
 279:         if resolution is None:
 280:             continue
 281:         if append_resolution(jsonl_path, resolution):
 282:             resolved_keys.add(key)
 283:             resolved_count += 1
 284: 
 285:     return resolved_count
 286: 
 287: 
 288: def main() -> int:
 289:     """Stand-alone invocation: bridge both postcommit + prepush JSONL files.
 290: 
 291:     Useful for ad-hoc invocation or as a SessionStart consumer. Stop-hook
 292:     wiring is via codex_stuck_detector.py importing bridge_unresolved_rows.
 293:     """
 294:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_REVIEW_THREAD_BRIDGE_DISABLE") == "1":
 295:         return 0
 296:     total = 0
 297:     for path in (_POSTCOMMIT_LOG, _PREPUSH_LOG):
 298:         total += bridge_unresolved_rows(path)
 299:     if total > 0:
 300:         # Surface to stderr for SessionStart additionalContext when wired
 301:         print(
 302:             f"codex_review_thread_bridge: resolved {total} thread_id mappings",
 303:             file=__import__("sys").stderr,
 304:         )
 305:     return 0
 306: 
 307: 
 308: if __name__ == "__main__":
 309:     raise SystemExit(main())

 130: _NOOP_BUCKET_DIR = _STATE_DIR / "codex_stop_review_gate_noop_buckets"
 131: _NOOP_BUCKET_FLUSH_INTERVAL_SEC = 300  # 5min flush window per codex prescribed_edit #5

 153: 
 154: def _resolve_schema_path() -> tuple[Path | None, str | None]:
 155:     """Lazy + fail-open schema-path resolution with repo-local primary.
 156: 
 157:     Cycle-after-909 third-fire fix-forward (codex T3 needs-attention conf=0.84
 158:     on 0fc2dc1a): iter-2's plugin-cache-only resolution silently DROPPED schema
 159:     enforcement under plugin skew, defeating the gate's purpose. This iter
 160:     vendors the schema to a stable repo-local path so --output-schema remains
 161:     available independent of plugin-cache version. Plugin-cache path retained
 162:     as DRIFT-DETECTION fallback only (separate audit-action-loop ship will
 163:     add SHA-mismatch alerting between vendored + cached versions).
 164: 
 165:     Resolution order:
 166:       1. PRIMARY: .claude/schemas/review-output.schema.json (repo-tracked)
 167:       2. FALLBACK: plugin cache (only if repo-local missing — degraded mode)
 168:       3. NEITHER: return (None, concrete_diag) — caller must treat as
 169:          distinct degraded gate result (block or skip), NOT run codex weakly.
 170: 
 171:     Returns (path, error_diag). Path is None ONLY when both repo-local and
 172:     plugin cache are unreachable; in that case caller treats as degraded.
 173:     """
 174:     if _REPO_LOCAL_SCHEMA.exists():
 175:         return (_REPO_LOCAL_SCHEMA, None)
 176:     # Repo-local missing — try plugin cache as DRIFT-DETECTION fallback.
 177:     # This branch should rarely fire after vendoring; if it does, surface as
 178:     # degraded telemetry so operators know schema is not the canonical source.
 179:     try:
 180:         sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
 181:         from _codex_plugin_root import resolve_openai_codex_plugin_root
 182:     except (ImportError, OSError) as exc:
 183:         return (None, f"schema_unavailable: repo-local missing + plugin_root_import_error: {exc.__class__.__name__}: {exc}")
 184:     try:
 185:         plugin_root = resolve_openai_codex_plugin_root()
 186:     except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — fail-open per cycle-279; helper may raise on version skew
 187:         return (None, f"schema_unavailable: repo-local missing + plugin_root_resolve_error: {exc.__class__.__name__}: {exc}")
 188:     plugin_schema = plugin_root / "schemas" / "review-output.schema.json"
 189:     if not plugin_schema.exists():
 190:         return (None, f"schema_unavailable: repo-local missing + plugin schema missing: {plugin_schema}")
 191:     return (plugin_schema, "degraded: using_plugin_cache_fallback (repo-local schema missing)")

 194:     ".claude/state/",
 195:     ".claude/projects/",
 196:     ".claude/plugins/cache/",
 197:     "node_modules/",
 198:     "reports/",
 199:     ".gitnexus/",
 200: )
 201: _EXCLUDE_SUFFIXES = (".log", ".jsonl")

 204: def _now_iso() -> str:
 205:     return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")  # noqa: UP017

 208: def _append_telemetry(row: dict) -> None:
 209:     try:
 210:         _STATE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 211:         with _TELEMETRY_JSONL.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
 212:             f.write(json.dumps(row) + "\n")
 213:     except OSError:
 214:         pass  # fail-open

 217: # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 218: # Ship #227 — noop count-bucket helpers (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.90).
 219: # Per-Stop-hook process dies after each invocation, so bucket state MUST be
 220: # durable on-disk (not in-memory). O_CREAT|O_EXCL lock + atomic temp-replace
 221: # prevents read-modify-write races across parallel Stop invocations. Sister
 222: # of Ship #220 (codex_t1_consult_gate.py:496-650) + Ship #222
 223: # (auto_proceed_gate.py:323-498), with 2 Ship #227-specific deltas:
 224: #   (a) _flush_all_due_noop_buckets scans every pending-*.json (not just
 225: #       current-hour) — fixes hour-boundary stranding bug Ship #220 has on
 226: #       sparse Stop hook fires (codex prescribed_edit #5).
 227: #   (b) bucket key includes dirty_files_count_bin per Q3 prescription
 228: #       (live distribution 261/223/32 across 0_5/6_20/21_plus bins).
 229: # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 680:     try:
 681:         result = subprocess.run(
 682:             [
 683:                 codex,
 684:                 "exec",
 685:                 "--ephemeral",
 686:                 "-p",
 687:                 # iter-30 profile unleash: deep-review (read-only) → deep-review-exec (workspace-write)
 688:                 # per user-explicit directive 2026-04-26 + rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md Pattern B
 689:                 # sub-cause 2 mitigation. T6 needs live worktree probe (uncommitted dirty-tree files)
 690:                 # which read-only sandbox rejects via pwsh.exe-based path.
 691:                 "deep-review-exec",
 692:                 "--skip-git-repo-check",
 693:                 "--color",
 694:                 "never",
 695:                 "--json",
 696:                 *schema_argv,
 697:                 "--output-last-message",
 698:                 str(out_path),
 699:                 "-",
 700:             ],
 701:             input=prompt,
 702:             cwd=str(_ROOT),
 703:             capture_output=True,
 704:             text=True,
 705:             timeout=timeout_sec,
 706:             check=False,
 707:             creationflags=creationflags,
 708:             startupinfo=startupinfo,
 709:             # iter-45 fix-forward: explicit CODEX_HOME prevents codex CLI from
 710:             # reading parent's ~/.codex/config.toml (which lacks
 711:             # `deep-review-exec` profile) when claude-sota's hook is invoked
 712:             # under ccc-mediated mode. Pattern B sub-cause 3 per
 713:             # rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md (n=2 codification).
 714:             env={**os.environ, "CODEX_HOME": str(_ROOT / ".codex")},
 715:         )
 716:     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
 717:         return ("", f"timeout_{timeout_sec:.0f}s")
 718:     except (FileNotFoundError, OSError) as exc:
 719:         return ("", f"spawn_error: {exc}")
 720: 
 721:     # Loud-fail per codex T1 revised plan step 4: capture rc + stderr tail when
 722:     # the last-message file is absent or codex exited non-zero. This replaces
 723:     # the prior silent fall-through where any failure became fail-open. Iter-2
 724:     # fix-forward: schema resolution failure (if any) prepended to diag for
 725:     # complete loud-fail trail — no silent degradation.
 726:     if not out_path.exists():
 727:         rc = result.returncode
 728:         stderr_tail = (result.stderr or "")[-200:].strip().replace("\n", " | ")
 729:         schema_prefix = f"schema_unavailable={schema_err!r} " if schema_err else ""
 730:         return ("", f"no_last_message_file (rc={rc}, stderr_tail={stderr_tail!r}, {schema_prefix})")
 731:     try:
 732:         # Iter-2 fix-forward: surface schema-unavailable in telemetry even on
 733:         # success path so degraded mode is visible (codex T3 fail-loud directive).
 734:         suffix = f" [schema_unavailable={schema_err!r}]" if schema_err else ""
 735:         return (out_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip(), suffix or None)
 736:     except OSError as exc:
 737:         return ("", f"read_error: {exc}")

 740: # ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 741: # Diff-hash dedupe (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.87 selected_option=B at
 742: # .claude/state/codex_consult_stop_gate_diff_hash_design_OUT.txt 2026-04-28)
 743: #
 744: # Purpose: skip codex spawn when the dirty tree is identical to a prior run
 745: # that returned a TRUSTED schema-valid approve. Reduces re-review of unchanged
 746: # state on repeated Stop attempts (live evidence: 2 fires at 15:11:45 +
 747: # 15:13:49 on identical 604-file dirty tree at sha 111c4b088875, ~16s combined
 748: # codex latency that dedupe skips in <100ms).
 749: #
 750: # Key invariants per codex T1 verdict:
 751: #   1. Persist allow ONLY when classification is TRUSTED (schema-valid approve
 752: #      with no contradictions). Empty/parse-error/unexpected/fail-open allow
 753: #      paths must NOT be persisted — would turn a loud fail-open into silent
 754: #      future dedupe.
 755: #   2. Skip dedupe when git_error is not None (partial-probe path falls
 756: #      through to review per AXIS-3-001 fail-loud).
 757: #   3. Skip dedupe in dry-run, no-files, and disabled paths (existing behavior
 758: #      stays ahead of dedupe).
 759: #   4. TTL bounds allow-skip lifetime against model/policy drift (60min default
 760: #      preserves same-turn relief, expires before long-session staleness).
 880:         stored_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(stored_ts.replace("Z", "+00:00"))
 881:     except (TypeError, ValueError):
 882:         return (False, None, "prior_ts_unparseable")
 883:     if stored_dt.tzinfo is None:
 884:         stored_dt = stored_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
 885:     age = (datetime.now(UTC) - stored_dt).total_seconds()
 886:     if age > _dedupe_ttl_sec():
 887:         return (False, None, f"ttl_expired_{age:.0f}s")
 888:     return (True, stored_verdict, None)
 889: 
 890: 
 891: def _persist_diff_hash(current_hash: str, verdict: str) -> None:
 892:     """Atomic write via unique temp + os.replace. Best-effort on failure.
 893: 
 894:     Per codex T1 AXIS-1 prescription: unique temp name (pid + uuid) prevents
 895:     concurrent writers corrupting each other's payload before rename.
 896:     """
 897:     if not current_hash:
 898:         return
 899:     final_path = _diff_hash_path()
 900:     tmp_path = (
 901:         _STATE_DIR
 902:         / f"codex_stop_review_last_diff_hash.{os.getpid()}.{uuid.uuid4().hex}.tmp"
 903:     )
 904:     payload = f"{current_hash}\n{verdict}\n{_now_iso()}\n"
 905:     try:
 906:         tmp_path.write_text(payload, encoding="utf-8")
 907:         os.replace(str(tmp_path), str(final_path))
 908:     except OSError:
 909:         with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
 910:             tmp_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 911: 
 912: 
 913: def _classify_verdict(last_msg: str) -> tuple[str, str, bool]:
 914:     """Inspect codex's schema-validated JSON output. Return (decision, reason, trusted_review).
 915: 
 916:     Cycle-after-909 second-fire migration: codex now emits JSON conforming to
 917:     `schemas/review-output.schema.json` (verdict enum: approve|needs-attention)
 918:     enforced via `--output-schema`. Map verdict='needs-attention' → 'block' and
 919:     verdict='approve' → 'allow'. Malformed/missing JSON or unexpected verdict
 920:     fails OPEN with a loud diagnostic — schema-enforced output should not
 921:     produce parse errors in practice; if it does, surface the cause rather
 922:     than silently treat as allow.
 923: 
 924:     Iter-4 fix-forward (codex T3 needs-attention conf=0.82 on 7fb3f703): the
 925:     upstream schema validates `verdict` and `findings` independently — a
 926:     semantically contradictory response (verdict=approve with non-empty
 927:     findings, or verdict=needs-attention with empty findings) passes structural
 928:     validation but is unsafe to consume verdict-only. Defensive rejection
 929:     chosen over schema-level conditional `if/then` to keep vendored schema
 930:     in sync with upstream plugin source (no divergence).
 931: 
 932:     Stop-gate-conservative-bias: contradictory response → BLOCK with concrete
 933:     diagnostic. Erring toward block is safe for sync Stop hook because user
 934:     has CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_DISABLE=1 escape hatch + can redact + re-run.
 935:     Silent allow on contradictory output would defeat the gate's purpose.
 936: 
 937:     Per codex T1 revised plan step 3: json.loads with concrete diagnostics.
 938: 
 939:     Cycle 2 ship (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION 0.87 AXIS-2 prescription): added
 940:     `trusted_review` boolean to the return tuple. trusted_review=True ONLY for
 941:     schema-valid {approve, needs-attention} verdicts that pass contradiction
 942:     checks. All fail-open allow paths (empty/parse-error/non-object/unexpected/
 943:     contradictory) return trusted_review=False so the dedupe layer in main()
 944:     can avoid persisting them as silent future skips.
 945:     """
 946:     if not last_msg:
 947:         return ("allow", "empty_response", False)
 948:     try:
 949:         parsed = json.loads(last_msg)
 950:     except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
 951:         return ("allow", f"json_parse_error: {exc.msg}", False)
 952:     if not isinstance(parsed, dict):
 953:         return ("allow", f"non_object_response: {type(parsed).__name__}", False)
 954:     verdict = parsed.get("verdict")
 955:     summary = str(parsed.get("summary", ""))[:300]
 956:     findings_raw = parsed.get("findings")
 957: 
 958:     # Iter-6 type-guard hardening (codex T3 needs-attention conf=0.78 on 092cb637,
 959:     # severity downgrade HIGH→MEDIUM = first severity decrement in 6-iter arc):
 960:     # The vendored schema (.claude/schemas/review-output.schema.json L6-11) declares
 961:     # findings as `required`. A final verdict payload with `findings: null` or with
 962:     # the `findings` key missing entirely violates the schema by definition.
 963:     # Iter-5's tolerance for None ("avoid false positives on partial output") was
 964:     # itself the silent-fallback the gate is meant to defend against — for FINAL
 965:     # verdict payloads (the only kind this gate consumes), require findings to be
 966:     # a list. `parsed.get("findings")` returns None for both `null` AND a missing
 967:     # key, so both fail-closed-loud BLOCK with concrete shape diagnostic.
 968:     if not isinstance(findings_raw, list):
 969:         return (
 970:             "block",
 971:             f"invalid_findings_shape_{type(findings_raw).__name__}: {summary or '(no summary)'}",
 972:             False,  # contradicts schema → not trusted
 973:         )
 974:     findings_count = len(findings_raw)  # always safe — guard above ensures list
 975: 
 976:     # Iter-4 contradiction guards: schema-valid but semantically unsafe shapes.
 977:     if verdict == "approve" and findings_count > 0:
 978:         return (
 979:             "block",
 980:             f"contradictory_approve_with_{findings_count}_findings: {summary or '(no summary)'}",
 981:             False,  # contradiction → block but NOT trusted; never persist as future-skip
 982:         )
 983:     if verdict == "needs-attention" and findings_count == 0:
 984:         return (
 985:             "block",
 986:             f"contradictory_needs_attention_with_no_findings: {summary or '(no summary)'}",
 987:             False,  # contradiction → block but NOT trusted
 988:         )
 989: 
 990:     # Normal verdict mapping (post-contradiction-check). trusted_review=True
 991:     # ONLY here — schema-valid + non-contradictory + recognized verdict.
 992:     if verdict == "needs-attention":
 993:         return ("block", summary or "needs-attention (no summary)", True)
 994:     if verdict == "approve":
 995:         return ("allow", summary or "approve (no summary)", True)
 996:     return ("allow", f"unexpected_verdict: {verdict!r}", False)
 997: 
 998: 
 999: def main() -> int:
1000:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_DISABLE") == "1":
1001:         return 0
1002:     # Ship #227.1 fix-forward (operational verification of Ship #227 caught P3
1003:     # bug — pending bucket files stranded between noop events because flush only
1004:     # fired inside _record_noop_bucket). Drain stranded pending buckets at the
1005:     # START of every Stop hook fire — non-noop fires (reviewed/fail_open/
1006:     # would_run/git_probe_error_partial) now also become flush opportunities.
1007:     # Defense-in-depth: in-_record_noop_bucket flush call is preserved for
1008:     # within-fire same-bucket emission; this start-of-main call covers
1009:     # cross-fire stranding from prior hours' noop activity.
1010:     _flush_all_due_noop_buckets(time.time())
1011:     # Ship #227 prescribed_edit #1: read stdin to extract agent_id/agent_type.
1012:     # Per SDK types.py:302-309: StopHookInput has only stop_hook_active;
1013:     # SubagentStopHookInput carries agent_id/agent_type. CHANGELOG.md:1360
1014:     # added agent_id/agent_type to hook events. Preserve when present.
1015:     event = _read_stdin()
1016:     agent_id = event.get("agent_id") if isinstance(event, dict) else None
1017:     agent_type = event.get("agent_type") if isinstance(event, dict) else None
1018:     started = time.time()
1019:     head_sha = _head_sha()
1020: 
1021:     files, git_error = _dirty_files()
1022: 
1023:     # AXIS-3-001 fail-loud (codex T1 5-axis P0 conf 0.92 verdict 2026-04-27):
1024:     # distinguish typed git_probe_error from genuine no_dirty_files. Prior code
1025:     # silently classified git failure (binary missing / nonzero / timeout /
1026:     # OSError) as `skipped_clean` decision=allow, masking the failure mode.
1027:     # STRICT (CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_STRICT=1) emits decision=block to stdout
1028:     # per Anthropic Stop hook spec; default WARN emits typed telemetry + allow
1029:     # so ccc operator surface stays usable while sss can opt-in to fail-loud.
1030:     if git_error is not None:
1031:         strict = os.environ.get("CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_STRICT") == "1"
1032:         # T3 fix-forward (iter-1.5 on commit 6d295a7): partial probe success
1033:         # (one git probe failed but another returned files) MUST fall through
1034:         # to review in WARN mode, NOT return decision=allow which suppresses
1035:         # review of known dirty files. Per codex T3 verdict on 88c9f358 HIGH
1036:         # conf 0.86: "Git probe errors suppress review of known dirty files
1037:         # in WARN mode". 3-way decision: STRICT always blocks; WARN+empty
1038:         # allows; WARN+non-empty falls through to review with warning attached.
1039:         decision = "block" if strict else ("allow" if not files else "continue_to_review")
1040:         status = "git_probe_error" if (strict or not files) else "git_probe_error_partial"
1041:         diag = f"git_probe_error:{git_error}"
1042:         _emit_telemetry(
1043:             {
1044:                 "ts": _now_iso(),
1045:                 "status": status,
1046:                 "git_error_class": git_error,
1047:                 "dirty_files_count": len(files),
1048:                 "decision": decision,
1049:                 "latency_ms": int((time.time() - started) * 1000),
1050:                 "sha_head": head_sha,
1051:                 "diag": diag,
1052:                 "strict_mode": strict,
1053:             },
1054:             agent_id=agent_id,
1055:             agent_type=agent_type,
1056:         )
1057:         if strict:
1058:             print(json.dumps({"decision": "block", "reason": diag}))
1059:             return 0
1060:         if not files:
1061:             return 0
1062:         # Fall through to review with partial dirty set; telemetry above
1063:         # records git_probe_error_partial so the partial-probe failure is
1064:         # visible alongside the review verdict.
1065: 
1066:     if not files:
1067:         _emit_telemetry(
1068:             {
1069:                 "ts": _now_iso(),
1070:                 "status": "skipped_clean",
1071:                 "dirty_files_count": 0,
1072:                 "decision": "allow",
1073:                 "latency_ms": int((time.time() - started) * 1000),
1074:                 "sha_head": head_sha,
1075:                 "diag": "no_dirty_files",
1076:             },
1077:             agent_id=agent_id,
1078:             agent_type=agent_type,
1079:         )
1080:         return 0
1081: 
1082:     if os.environ.get("CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_DRY_RUN") == "1":
1083:         _emit_telemetry(
1084:             {
1085:                 "ts": _now_iso(),
1086:                 "status": "would_run",
1087:                 "dirty_files_count": len(files),
1088:                 "decision": "allow",
1089:                 "latency_ms": int((time.time() - started) * 1000),
1090:                 "sha_head": head_sha,
1091:                 "diag": f"dry_run files={files[:5]}",
1092:             },
1093:             agent_id=agent_id,
1094:             agent_type=agent_type,
1095:         )
1096:         return 0
1097: 
1098:     # Diff-hash dedupe (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.87 selected_option=B).
1099:     # Placement per AXIS-2 prescription: AFTER git_error/clean/dry-run branches,
1100:     # BEFORE the expensive _build_diff_payload + _invoke_codex_review path. Skip
1101:     # entirely when git_error is not None (partial-probe path must reach codex).
1102:     current_hash = ""
1103:     dedupe_miss_reason: str | None = None
1104:     if git_error is None:
1105:         current_hash = _compute_diff_hash(files, head_sha)
1106:         skip, prior_decision, dedupe_miss_reason = _check_dedupe(current_hash)
1107:         if skip:
1108:             _emit_telemetry(
1109:                 {
1110:                     "ts": _now_iso(),
1111:                     "status": "dedupe_skip",
1112:                     "dirty_files_count": len(files),
1113:                     "decision": prior_decision or "allow",
1114:                     "latency_ms": int((time.time() - started) * 1000),
1115:                     "sha_head": head_sha,
1116:                     "diag": f"dedupe_hit hash={current_hash[:16]}",
1117:                     "diff_hash_prefix": current_hash[:16],
1118:                 },
1119:                 agent_id=agent_id,
1120:                 agent_type=agent_type,
1121:             )
1122:             # Stop allow on dedupe: no stdout JSON emitted (default-allow Stop).
1123:             return 0
1124: 
1125:     diff_body = _build_diff_payload(files)
1126:     last_msg, err = _invoke_codex_review(diff_body, _timeout_sec())
1127:     if err:
1128:         # Fail-open: codex unavailable / timeout / spawn-error → allow stop.
1129:         # Per codex T1 AXIS-2: do NOT persist this allow into dedupe state —
1130:         # would convert loud fail-open into silent future skip on same tree.
1131:         _emit_telemetry(
1132:             {
1133:                 "ts": _now_iso(),
1134:                 "status": "fail_open",
1135:                 "dirty_files_count": len(files),
1136:                 "decision": "allow",
1137:                 "latency_ms": int((time.time() - started) * 1000),
1138:                 "sha_head": head_sha,
1139:                 "diag": err,
1140:                 "trusted_review": False,
1141:             },
1142:             agent_id=agent_id,
1143:             agent_type=agent_type,
1144:         )
1145:         return 0
1146: 
1147:     decision, reason, trusted_review = _classify_verdict(last_msg)
1148:     _emit_telemetry(
1149:         {
1150:             "ts": _now_iso(),
1151:             "status": "reviewed",

 232: def _date_hour(now: datetime | None = None) -> str:
 233:     """UTC YYYY-MM-DDTHH bucket key (1-hour aggregation window)."""
 234:     ts = now if now is not None else datetime.now(UTC)
 235:     return ts.astimezone(UTC).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H")
 236: 
 237: 
 238: def _bucket_lock_path(date_hour: str) -> Path:
 239:     return _NOOP_BUCKET_DIR / f"lock-{date_hour}.lock"
 240: 
 241: 
 242: def _bucket_pending_path(date_hour: str) -> Path:
 243:     return _NOOP_BUCKET_DIR / f"pending-{date_hour}.json"
 244: 
 245: 
 246: def _with_bucket_lock(lock_path: Path, fn) -> bool:
 247:     """Execute fn() under O_CREAT|O_EXCL lock; bounded retry (5s deadline / 50ms backoff)."""
 248:     deadline = time.time() + 5.0
 249:     while time.time() < deadline:
 250:         try:
 251:             lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 252:             fd = os.open(str(lock_path), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY, 0o644)
 253:             try:
 254:                 os.write(fd, str(os.getpid()).encode("utf-8"))
 255:             finally:
 256:                 os.close(fd)
 257:             try:
 258:                 fn()
 259:             finally:
 260:                 with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
 261:                     lock_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 262:             return True
 263:         except FileExistsError:
 264:             time.sleep(0.05)
 265:         except OSError:
 266:             return False
 267:     return False
 268: 
 269: 
 270: def _empty_pending_bucket(date_hour: str) -> dict:
 271:     return {
 272:         "schema": _TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_BUCKET_PENDING,
 273:         "date_hour": date_hour,
 274:         "first_ts": None,
 275:         "last_ts": None,
 276:         "entries": [],
 277:     }
 278: 
 279: 
 280: def _load_pending_bucket(path: Path, date_hour: str) -> dict:
 281:     if not path.is_file():
 282:         return _empty_pending_bucket(date_hour)
 283:     try:
 284:         with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
 285:             data = json.load(fh)
 286:         if isinstance(data, dict) and isinstance(data.get("entries"), list):
 287:             return data
 288:     except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
 289:         pass
 290:     return _empty_pending_bucket(date_hour)
 291: 
 292: 
 293: def _write_pending_bucket_atomic(path: Path, bucket: dict) -> None:
 294:     path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 295:     tmp = path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".tmp")
 296:     try:
 297:         with tmp.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
 298:             json.dump(bucket, fh, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)
 299:         os.replace(str(tmp), str(path))
 300:     except OSError:
 301:         with contextlib.suppress(OSError):
 302:             tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 303: 
 304: 
 305: def _latency_histogram_bin(latency_ms: int) -> str:
 306:     """Codex prescribed_edit #4: 4-bin fixed histogram preserves regression signal.
 307: 
 308:     Sister of Ship #222 auto_proceed_gate.py:391 (verbatim bin boundaries).
 309:     """
 310:     if latency_ms < 100:
 311:         return "lt100"
 312:     if latency_ms < 500:
 313:         return "100_499"
 314:     if latency_ms < 1000:
 315:         return "500_999"
 316:     return "gte1000"
 317: 
 318: 
 319: def _dirty_files_count_bin(count: int) -> str:
 320:     """Codex Q3: low-cardinality bin for dedupe_skip dirty_files_count.
 321: 
 322:     Live empirical distribution (codex T1 probe 2026-05-02): 261/223/32 across
 323:     0_5/6_20/21_plus respectively — meaningful + low-cardinality preserves
 324:     regression signal that pure-omit would lose.
 325:     """
 326:     if count <= 5:
 327:         return "0_5"
 328:     if count <= 20:
 329:         return "6_20"
 330:     return "21_plus"
 331: 
 332: 
 333: def _noop_reason_for_status(status: str) -> str:
 334:     """Codex prescribed_edit #3: normalized noop_reason class (NOT dedupe_miss_reason).
 335: 
 336:     REFUTED my draft Q4: dedupe_skip is a dedupe HIT (carries diag "dedupe_hit hash=...");
 337:     the dedupe_miss_reason field belongs only on reviewed v1 rows.
 338:     """
 339:     if status == "skipped_clean":
 340:         return "no_dirty_files"
 341:     if status == "dedupe_skip":
 342:         return "dedupe_hit"
 343:     return "unknown"  # defensive — unreachable by _NOOP_BUCKETABLE_STATUSES filter
 344: 
 345: 
 346: def _increment_noop_bucket(bucket: dict, entry: dict) -> dict:
 347:     """Find matching entry by 7-tuple key; aggregate latency separately.
 348: 
 349:     Bucket key per codex prescribed_edit #3 + Q3:
 350:       (status, decision, noop_reason, dirty_files_count_bin, session_id, agent_id, agent_type)
 351:     Latency aggregated as count/sum/min/max + 4-bin histogram per #4.
 352:     """
 353:     key_fields = (
 354:         "status", "decision", "noop_reason", "dirty_files_count_bin",
 355:         "session_id", "agent_id", "agent_type",
 356:     )
 357:     new_key = tuple(entry.get(k) for k in key_fields)
 358:     ts = entry["ts"]
 359:     latency = int(entry.get("latency_ms", 0))
 360:     bucket["last_ts"] = ts
 361:     if bucket["first_ts"] is None:
 362:         bucket["first_ts"] = ts
 363:     bin_key = _latency_histogram_bin(latency)
 364:     for existing in bucket["entries"]:
 365:         if tuple(existing.get(k) for k in key_fields) == new_key:
 366:             existing["count"] = existing.get("count", 0) + 1
 367:             existing["last_ts"] = ts
 368:             existing["latency_ms_count"] = existing.get("latency_ms_count", 0) + 1
 369:             existing["latency_ms_sum"] = existing.get("latency_ms_sum", 0) + latency
 370:             existing["latency_ms_min"] = min(existing.get("latency_ms_min", latency), latency)
 371:             existing["latency_ms_max"] = max(existing.get("latency_ms_max", latency), latency)
 372:             hist = existing.setdefault(
 373:                 "latency_ms_histogram",
 374:                 {"lt100": 0, "100_499": 0, "500_999": 0, "gte1000": 0},
 375:             )
 376:             hist[bin_key] = hist.get(bin_key, 0) + 1
 377:             return bucket
 378:     new_entry: dict = {k: entry.get(k) for k in key_fields}
 379:     new_hist = {"lt100": 0, "100_499": 0, "500_999": 0, "gte1000": 0}
 380:     new_hist[bin_key] = 1
 381:     new_entry["count"] = 1
 382:     new_entry["first_ts"] = ts
 383:     new_entry["last_ts"] = ts
 384:     new_entry["latency_ms_count"] = 1
 385:     new_entry["latency_ms_sum"] = latency
 386:     new_entry["latency_ms_min"] = latency
 387:     new_entry["latency_ms_max"] = latency
 388:     new_entry["latency_ms_histogram"] = new_hist
 389:     bucket["entries"].append(new_entry)
 390:     return bucket
 391:

 393: def _flush_pending_bucket_file(pending_path: Path) -> None:
 394:     """Idempotent: load → emit BUCKET schema row → unlink. Lock acquired by caller."""
 395:     if not pending_path.is_file():
 396:         return
 397:     try:
 398:         with pending_path.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
 399:             bucket = json.load(fh)
 400:     except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
 401:         return
 402:     flushed = {
 403:         "schema": _TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_BUCKET,
 404:         "ts": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
 405:         "date_hour": bucket.get("date_hour"),
 406:         "first_ts": bucket.get("first_ts"),
 407:         "last_ts": bucket.get("last_ts"),
 408:         "entries": bucket.get("entries", []),
 409:     }
 410:     try:
 411:         _STATE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 412:         with _TELEMETRY_JSONL.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
 413:             fh.write(json.dumps(flushed, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
 414:         pending_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 415:     except OSError:
 416:         pass
 417: 
 418: 
 419: def _flush_all_due_noop_buckets(now_ts: float) -> None:
 420:     """Scan every pending-*.json older than _NOOP_BUCKET_FLUSH_INTERVAL_SEC and flush.
 421: 
 422:     Codex prescribed_edit #5: Ship #220's current-hour-only flush leaves
 423:     pending-YYYY-MM-DDTHH.json stranded if next noop fires in a later hour.
 424:     Stop hook fires ~50/day (sparse) so this risk is real here in a way it
 425:     wasn't for Ship #220's ~100/day codex_t1_consult_gate.
 426:     """
 427:     if not _NOOP_BUCKET_DIR.is_dir():
 428:         return
 429:     try:
 430:         candidates = list(_NOOP_BUCKET_DIR.glob("pending-*.json"))
 431:     except OSError:
 432:         return
 433:     for pending_path in candidates:
 434:         try:
 435:             mtime = pending_path.stat().st_mtime
 436:         except OSError:
 437:             continue
 438:         if now_ts - mtime < _NOOP_BUCKET_FLUSH_INTERVAL_SEC:
 439:             continue
 440:         # Extract date_hour from filename: pending-YYYY-MM-DDTHH.json → YYYY-MM-DDTHH
 441:         date_hour = pending_path.stem[len("pending-"):]
 442:         lock_path = _bucket_lock_path(date_hour)
 443:         _with_bucket_lock(lock_path, lambda p=pending_path: _flush_pending_bucket_file(p))
 444: 
 445: 
 446: def _record_noop_bucket(record: dict) -> None:
 447:     """Update on-disk pending bucket atomically; schedule due-bucket flushes.
 448: 
 449:     Caller passes a v1-shaped record with status/decision/dirty_files_count/latency_ms
 450:     plus session_id/agent_id/agent_type. This helper normalizes noop_reason +
 451:     dirty_files_count_bin and routes to the bucket entry.
 452:     """
 453:     now = datetime.now(UTC)
 454:     date_hour = _date_hour(now)
 455:     pending_path = _bucket_pending_path(date_hour)
 456:     lock_path = _bucket_lock_path(date_hour)
 457:     entry = dict(record)
 458:     entry["ts"] = now.isoformat(timespec="seconds")
 459:     entry["noop_reason"] = _noop_reason_for_status(record.get("status", ""))
 460:     entry["dirty_files_count_bin"] = _dirty_files_count_bin(
 461:         int(record.get("dirty_files_count", 0))
 462:     )
 463: 
 464:     def _do_update():
 465:         bucket = _load_pending_bucket(pending_path, date_hour)
 466:         bucket = _increment_noop_bucket(bucket, entry)
 467:         _write_pending_bucket_atomic(pending_path, bucket)
 468: 
 469:     _with_bucket_lock(lock_path, _do_update)
 470:     _flush_all_due_noop_buckets(time.time())
 471:

 473: def _emit_telemetry(record: dict, *, agent_id=None, agent_type=None) -> None:
 474:     """Schema-aware dispatch (codex prescribed_edit #2): noop subset → bucket; rest → v1 full row.
 475: 
 476:     Per #1: agent_id/agent_type propagated into BOTH paths from main()'s stdin
 477:     parse. Per audit-action-loop.md SDK contract — preserve when present, do not
 478:     fabricate (Stop payloads do not always carry them per types.py:302).
 479:     """
 480:     enriched = dict(record)
 481:     if agent_id is not None:
 482:         enriched["agent_id"] = agent_id
 483:     if agent_type is not None:
 484:         enriched["agent_type"] = agent_type
 485:     if record.get("status") in _NOOP_BUCKETABLE_STATUSES:
 486:         _record_noop_bucket(enriched)
 487:     else:
 488:         enriched.setdefault("schema", _TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_FULL)
 489:         _append_telemetry(enriched)

 492: def _read_stdin() -> dict:
 493:     try:
 494:         if sys.stdin.isatty():
 495:             return {}
 496:         raw = sys.stdin.read()
 497:         if not raw.strip():
 498:             return {}
 499:         parsed = json.loads(raw)
 500:         return parsed if isinstance(parsed, dict) else {}
 501:     except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError, ValueError):
 502:         return {}

 505: def _resolve_cmd(name: str) -> str | None:
 506:     return shutil.which(name)

 508: 
 509: def _git_lines(args: list[str]) -> tuple[list[str], str | None]:
 510:     """Run `git <args>` and return (lines, error_class).
 511: 
 512:     AXIS-3-001 fail-loud fix (codex T1 5-axis P0 conf 0.92 verdict
 513:     2026-04-27): classifies probe failures as typed errors instead of
 514:     silently returning empty list. Cardinal: "no silent fallback".
 515: 
 516:     error_class is None on success; otherwise one of:
 517:       git_missing            — `git` binary not on PATH
 518:       git_returncode_<N>     — non-zero exit (e.g. not a repo, bad ref)
 519:       git_timeout            — subprocess timeout (10s)
 520:       git_oserror_<Type>     — OSError class name (e.g. PermissionError)

 succeeded in 368ms:
 520:       git_oserror_<Type>     — OSError class name (e.g. PermissionError)
 521: 
 522:     Caller (main) distinguishes git_probe_error from no_dirty_files; STRICT
 523:     (CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_STRICT=1) emits decision=block to stdout per
 524:     Anthropic Stop hook spec; default WARN emits telemetry + allow.
 525:     """
 526:     git = _resolve_cmd("git")
 527:     if not git:
 528:         return [], "git_missing"
 529:     try:
 530:         r = subprocess.run(
 531:             [git, *args],
 532:             cwd=str(_ROOT),
 533:             capture_output=True,
 534:             text=True,
 535:             timeout=10.0,
 536:             check=False,
 537:         )
 538:         if r.returncode != 0:
 539:             return [], f"git_returncode_{r.returncode}"
 540:         return [ln.strip() for ln in (r.stdout or "").splitlines() if ln.strip()], None
 541:     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
 542:         return [], "git_timeout"
 543:     except OSError as e:
 544:         return [], f"git_oserror_{type(e).__name__}"

 547: def _is_excluded(rel_path: str) -> bool:
 548:     rp = rel_path.replace("\\", "/")
 549:     return any(rp.startswith(p) for p in _EXCLUDE_PREFIXES) or any(
 550:         rp.endswith(s) for s in _EXCLUDE_SUFFIXES
 551:     )

 553: 
 554: def _dirty_files() -> tuple[list[str], str | None]:
 555:     """Return (deduped_files, first_error_class). Filters _is_excluded paths.
 556: 
 557:     AXIS-3-001 fail-loud: error class propagates from _git_lines so main()
 558:     distinguishes git_probe_error (typed) from no_dirty_files (genuine clean).
 559:     First non-None error wins; subsequent probes still attempt so partial
 560:     success surfaces what files we DID see alongside the error class.
 561:     """
 562:     seen: set[str] = set()
 563:     error_class: str | None = None
 564:     for args in (
 565:         ["diff", "--name-only", "HEAD"],
 566:         ["diff", "--cached", "--name-only"],
 567:         ["ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
 568:     ):
 569:         lines, err = _git_lines(args)
 570:         if err is not None and error_class is None:
 571:             error_class = err
 572:         seen.update(lines)
 573:     return sorted(p for p in seen if not _is_excluded(p)), error_class
 574:

 576: def _head_sha() -> str:
 577:     lines, _err = _git_lines(["rev-parse", "--short=12", "HEAD"])
 578:     return lines[0] if lines else "unknown"

 581: def _timeout_sec() -> float:
 582:     raw = os.environ.get("CODEX_STOP_REVIEW_GATE_TIMEOUT_SEC", "").strip()
 583:     if not raw:
 584:         return _DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SEC
 585:     try:
 586:         n = float(raw)
 587:         return n if n > 0 else _DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SEC
 588:     except ValueError:
 589:         return _DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SEC

 592: def _build_diff_payload(files: list[str]) -> str:
 593:     """Produce the diff body codex reviews. Caps file count to keep prompt bounded."""
 594:     git = _resolve_cmd("git")
 595:     if not git or not files:
 596:         return ""
 597:     capped = files[:30]
 598:     chunks: list[str] = [f"# Worktree dirty files ({len(files)} total, showing first {len(capped)}):\n"]
 599:     chunks.extend(f"#   {p}\n" for p in capped)
 600:     chunks.append("\n# Diff (HEAD..worktree, capped):\n")
 601:     try:
 602:         r = subprocess.run(
 603:             [git, "diff", "HEAD", "--", *capped],
 604:             cwd=str(_ROOT),
 605:             capture_output=True,
 606:             text=True,
 607:             timeout=15.0,
 608:             check=False,
 609:         )
 610:         chunks.append((r.stdout or "")[:50_000])  # cap diff body 50K chars
 611:     except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):
 612:         chunks.append("# (diff capture failed)\n")
 613:     return "".join(chunks)

 616: def _invoke_codex_review(diff_body: str, timeout_sec: float) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
 617:     """Spawn codex deep-review on the diff. Returns (last_message_text, error).
 618: 
 619:     Cycle-after-909 second-fire: migrated to schema-validated output. Codex now
 620:     enforces `verdict ∈ {approve, needs-attention}` per
 621:     `schemas/review-output.schema.json`; parser maps approve→allow and
 622:     needs-attention→block. Schema rejection or malformed JSON surfaces in
 623:     telemetry as fail-loud cause, not silent fallback.
 624:     """
 625:     codex = _resolve_cmd("codex")
 626:     if not codex:
 627:         return ("", "codex_not_found")
 628:     out_path = _STATE_DIR / f"codex_stop_review_gate_{int(time.time())}_{os.getpid()}.txt"
 629:     prompt = (
 630:         "You are a stop-gate reviewer. The worktree has uncommitted changes the user is "
 631:         "about to leave behind by closing this Claude Code session. Decide whether the "
 632:         "session should be BLOCKED from closing pending review, or ALLOWED to close.\n\n"
 633:         "BLOCK (return verdict='needs-attention') only if the worktree contains: "
 634:         "(a) introduced secrets/credentials, (b) destructive ops without test coverage, "
 635:         "(c) clear bugs in safety-critical code paths. Otherwise ALLOW (verdict='approve').\n\n"
 636:         "Return ONLY a JSON object conforming to the schema given via --output-schema. "
 637:         "The 'summary' field carries your one-sentence rationale; 'findings' may be empty "
 638:         "for approve, or list block-reasons for needs-attention; 'next_steps' may be empty.\n\n"
 639:         "Below is the diff:\n\n" + diff_body
 640:     )
 641:     # Windows console-suppression — SOTA pattern grounded in openai/codex-plugin-cc
 642:     # official source at .claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.4/scripts/lib/
 643:     # app-server.mjs:194 + process.mjs:13 which both pass `windowsHide: true` to
 644:     # node's spawn(). windowsHide maps directly to Windows STARTUPINFO with
 645:     # STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW + wShowWindow=SW_HIDE — the canonical Windows API to
 646:     # force-hide child console windows even if the child binary tries to allocate
 647:     # one anyway. Combine with CREATE_NO_WINDOW for belt-and-suspenders coverage.
 648:     #
 649:     # User-reported regression 2026-04-26 (commit 9e7124b8 was insufficient):
 650:     # CREATE_NO_WINDOW alone doesn't suppress the rust codex.exe binary's own
 651:     # console allocation when spawned through node.js wrapper. The grandchild
 652:     # rust binary inherits its own window-allocation behavior; STARTUPINFO+SW_HIDE
 653:     # is the SOTA fix per official OpenAI plugin source.
 654:     # Iter-3 fix-forward (codex T3 needs-attention conf=0.84 on 0fc2dc1a):
 655:     # vendored repo-local schema means --output-schema enforcement is now
 656:     # independent of plugin-cache version skew. If even repo-local schema is
 657:     # missing (developer accidentally deleted .claude/schemas/), schema_path
 658:     # is None — treat as DISTINCT degraded gate result per codex's option 1:
 659:     # do NOT invoke codex without --output-schema, since unstructured output
 660:     # would let real needs-attention slip through. Skip to fail-open allow
 661:     # with explicit degraded-mode telemetry (gate is non-authoritative).
 662:     schema_path, schema_err = _resolve_schema_path()
 663:     if schema_path is None:
 664:         return ("", f"schema_unavailable_gate_skipped: {schema_err}")
 665:     schema_argv = ["--output-schema", str(schema_path)]
 666:     creationflags = 0
 667:     startupinfo = None
 668:     if sys.platform == "win32":
 669:         # mypy-canonical pattern (python/mypy dmypy_server.py:46-69 @ 614b757c):
 670:         # CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE pre-allocates a HIDDEN console for direct child
 671:         # (node.exe), which Rust grandchild (codex.exe) INHERITS via Node's
 672:         # stdio:"inherit" spawn — closes n=4+ user-reported visible window.
 673:         # Switch from 0x08000000 (CREATE_NO_WINDOW, only applies to direct
 674:         # child) per cpython#4150 eryksun + mypy PR#5994 (production 2018).
 675:         # Mirror of parent fix.
 676:         creationflags = 0x10  # CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE (was 0x08000000)
 677:         startupinfo = subprocess.STARTUPINFO()
 678:         startupinfo.dwFlags |= subprocess.STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW
 679:         startupinfo.wShowWindow = subprocess.SW_HIDE
 680:     try:

 succeeded in 332ms:
 760: #      preserves same-turn relief, expires before long-session staleness).
 761: #   5. Hash includes head_sha + sorted path list + per-file content sha256
 762: #      (Option B; Option A misses content edits, Option C is too lossy for
 763: #      the synchronous T6 gate that includes untracked files).

 786: 
 787: def _file_marker(rel_path: str) -> tuple[str, bytes]:
 788:     """Return (marker, content_sha256_bytes) for a dirty path.
 789: 
 790:     Per codex T1 AXIS-3 prescription: deleted/missing/directory/binary/
 791:     unreadable paths get a deterministic marker so states do not collapse to
 792:     only path+head — a deleted file MUST hash differently from a present file.
 793:     """
 794:     abs_path = _ROOT / rel_path
 795:     try:
 796:         st = abs_path.stat() if abs_path.exists() else None
 797:     except OSError as exc:
 798:         return (f"unreadable:{type(exc).__name__}", b"")
 799:     if st is None:
 800:         return ("missing", b"")
 801:     if abs_path.is_dir():
 802:         return ("dir", b"")
 803:     if not abs_path.is_file():
 804:         return ("nonfile", b"")
 805:     try:
 806:         content_hasher = hashlib.sha256()
 807:         with abs_path.open("rb") as f:
 808:             while True:
 809:                 chunk = f.read(_HASH_CHUNK_SIZE)
 810:                 if not chunk:
 811:                     break
 812:                 content_hasher.update(chunk)
 813:         return ("file", content_hasher.digest())
 814:     except (OSError, PermissionError) as exc:
 815:         return (f"unreadable:{type(exc).__name__}", b"")
 816: 
 817: 
 818: def _compute_diff_hash(files: list[str], head_sha: str) -> str:
 819:     """Option B content-aware hash. Returns full sha256 hex (64 char).
 820: 
 821:     Wire format uses unambiguous delimiters (NUL bytes) and explicit lengths
 822:     so 'a\x00b' and 'ab' cannot collide. Returns empty string only for empty
 823:     file list — a dirty tree with unreadable files still produces a non-empty
 824:     hash with unreadable markers (per codex T1 AXIS-3 prescription).
 825:     """
 826:     if not files:
 827:         return ""
 828:     h = hashlib.sha256()
 829:     h.update(b"head:")
 830:     h.update(head_sha.encode("utf-8"))
 831:     h.update(b"\x00")
 832:     for path in sorted(files):
 833:         marker, content = _file_marker(path)
 834:         h.update(marker.encode("utf-8"))
 835:         h.update(b":")
 836:         h.update(path.encode("utf-8"))
 837:         h.update(b"\x00")
 838:         h.update(len(content).to_bytes(8, "big"))
 839:         h.update(content)
 840:         h.update(b"\x01")
 841:     return h.hexdigest()
 842: 
 843: 
 844: def _check_dedupe(current_hash: str) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str | None]:
 845:     """Return (skip, prior_decision, reason_if_no_skip).
 846: 
 847:     Skip ONLY when ALL of:
 848:       - dedupe not disabled via env
 849:       - current_hash non-empty
 850:       - prior record exists, parses cleanly (3+ lines)
 851:       - stored hash matches current
 852:       - stored verdict == 'allow' (BLOCK never dedupes per codex T1 AXIS-2)
 853:       - age within TTL
 854: 
 855:     Per codex T1 AXIS-2 prescription: any unparseable / mismatched / non-allow
 856:     / expired record falls through with reason logged for telemetry.
 857:     """
 858:     if not current_hash:
 859:         return (False, None, "no_dirty_files_pre_hash")
 860:     if os.environ.get(_DEDUPE_DISABLE_ENV, "").strip():
 861:         return (False, None, "dedupe_disabled_env")
 862:     path = _diff_hash_path()
 863:     if not path.exists():
 864:         return (False, None, "no_prior_record")
 865:     try:
 866:         text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
 867:     except OSError:
 868:         return (False, None, "prior_record_unreadable")
 869:     lines = text.strip().split("\n")
 870:     if len(lines) < 3:
 871:         return (False, None, "prior_record_malformed")
 872:     stored_hash = lines[0].strip()
 873:     stored_verdict = lines[1].strip()
 874:     stored_ts = lines[2].strip()
 875:     if stored_hash != current_hash:
 876:         return (False, None, "hash_mismatch")
 877:     if stored_verdict != "allow":
 878:         return (False, None, f"prior_verdict_was_{stored_verdict}")
 879:     try:
 880:         stored_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(stored_ts.replace("Z", "+00:00"))

**Evaluating documentation issues**

I see some potential issues with the A2 port-note, which says `_guard_base NOT present`, even though it is. This might mean the documentation is outdated, potentially a P3 finding. 

Regarding the A4 wire documents, using `"timeout": 5000` could confuse users into thinking it's 5000 seconds, not 5. Also, there's a question about matcher compatibility. User's asking for proper configuration, so I might need to cite specific lines and clarify if the matcher supports pipe or if it only operates on certain events like PostToolUse.
**Analyzing SubagentStop handling**

The user asked about SubagentStop, but it seems like the code only processes PostToolUse and doesn't consider the SubagentStop payload, which is concerning. Without the appropriate payload structure, like tool_name being absent for SubagentStop, it could lead to missing telemetry. The documentation only refers to PostToolUse with Bash or Agent, not SubagentStop.

There might be a gap in readiness for wiring SubagentStop, unless changes are made to accommodate it. Additionally, A4’s `_is_codex_command` behavior could miss various command formats, potentially impacting PowerShell usage.
**Considering command execution paths**

I’m realizing that using `codex exec --ephemeral` might be done directly, as `shutil.which codex` can provide the path for A5. However, A4 appears to be tracing the Bash command as it’s entered by the user. This discrepancy could lead to confusion on how commands are processed. I need to ensure I clarify whether these commands are being interpreted correctly in both contexts, especially if paths might change with different executions.
**Considering sync and async hooks**

I’m thinking about how the sync and async hooks work when they stop at the same event. It seems I need to figure out the order of these hooks. The existing settings indicate that the Stop hooks are in an array. If A5 sync is inserted before the async one, I wonder if these codes run in a sequential way? It feels like I should infer that. I should check the user's official documents directly for clarity since they want that.
**Evaluating process IDs and risks**

I’m considering the implications of reusing a PID after more than 10 minutes, especially if the output file is small or missing—it might inadvertently terminate an unrelated process. I need to assess the risk of old PID reuse properly. Since on Windows, PIDs can be recycled, I'm curious if log rows show the process creation time. Unfortunately, they don’t seem to, and it looks like `_pid_alive` only relates to using OpenProcess.
**Considering duplicate telemetry**

I’m thinking about the duplicates in race data. Consumers might join by stream_file and just deal with the duplicates, but that could lead to issues with sending duplicate telemetry. It makes me wonder if a locking mechanism around the bridge is necessary, but it doesn’t seem too severe overall. I notice that the A5 telemetry bucket already uses a lock, which makes me think about consistency in managing this issue.

  13: # Reference (PARENT HARNESS PROVENANCE — TIER-3, not SOTA authority): Z:/claude/hooks/CLAUDE.md (parent harness Invocation classes table —
  14: #   Class 3 library helper convention with `_` prefix per cycle-263)
  15: # Source classification: LOCAL DESIGN-NOVEL for claude-sota; broker thread 019dc541-7cb5-7b53-93e2-de98cf693ccf is TIER-3 evidence trail, not source authority
  16: #   (cycle-590 ship 6 GPT-5.5 self-audit P2 finding -> ship 8 resolver implementation)
  17: """Library helper: resolve active openai-codex plugin install directory.
  18: 
  19: Cycle-590 ship 8 — closes codex GPT-5.5 self-audit P2 finding (broker
  20: thread 019dc541-7cb5-7b53-93e2-de98cf693ccf, cycle-590 ship 6 self-audit):
  21: 
  22:   [medium] P2: Review hooks are pinned to plugin cache version 1.0.4
  23:   (hooks/codex_postcommit_review.py:153-155). Both auto-review hooks
  24:   hard-code `.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.4` for the
  25:   schema and adversarial-review prompt. A plugin update, cache cleanup,
  26:   or side-by-side version can silently strand postcommit/prepush review
  27:   on stale or missing files; this also fights the `/reload-plugins`
  28:   workflow because the hooks do not resolve the active plugin version
  29:   dynamically.
  30: 
  31:   Recommendation: Add a shared `resolve_openai_codex_plugin_root()`
  32:   helper with env override, active-plugin manifest lookup if available,
  33:   and newest-version glob fallback; use it in postcommit, prepush, and
  34:   trace citations. Estimate: 35-55 LOC plus tests for missing/multiple
  35:   versions.
  36: 
  37: Resolution order (highest precedence first):
  38: 
  39: 1. **CODEX_PLUGIN_ROOT** env override — manual pin to a specific
  40:    directory. Useful for: smoke-testing against a different version,
  41:    bisecting upstream breakage, vendoring an unpublished build.
  42: 2. **Newest version** under `.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/`
  43:    sorted by SemVer int-tuple key (so `1.0.10 > 1.0.4 > 1.0.3`,
  44:    correcting the lexicographic-sort pitfall that would otherwise place
  45:    `1.0.10` before `1.0.4`).
  46: 3. **Fail-open hardcoded baseline** `1.0.4` — preserves cycle-590
  47:    pre-ship-8 behavior so a missing/empty plugin cache doesn't block
  48:    hook execution. The hook will then fail at file-open in `_launch_review`
  49:    per its existing fail-open semantics.
  50: 
  51: Per cycle-263 `_` prefix convention for library helpers.
  52: Per Class 3 in `hooks/CLAUDE.md` Invocation classes table.
  53: """
  54: 
  55: from __future__ import annotations
  56: 
  57: import os
  58: from pathlib import Path
  59: 
  60: _REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
  61: _BASE_PATH = _REPO_ROOT / ".claude" / "plugins" / "cache" / "openai-codex" / "codex"
  62: _FALLBACK_VERSION = "1.0.4"
  63: _ENV_OVERRIDE = "CODEX_PLUGIN_ROOT"
  64: 
  65: 
  66: def _version_key(path: Path) -> tuple[int, ...]:
  67:     """Parse version-dir name as int tuple for proper SemVer ordering.
  68: 
  69:     `Path('1.0.10').name.split('.')` → `('1','0','10')` → `(1, 0, 10)`,
  70:     which sorts after `(1, 0, 4)` as expected. Lexicographic string sort
  71:     would incorrectly put `'1.0.10' < '1.0.4'` because `'1' < '4'` at
  72:     the third position. Falls back to `(0,)` for non-numeric parts so
  73:     a stray non-version dir name doesn't crash the sort.
  74:     """
  75:     try:
  76:         parts: list[int] = []
  77:         for part in path.name.split("."):
  78:             if part.isdigit():
  79:                 parts.append(int(part))
  80:             else:
  81:                 # Stop at first non-numeric segment (e.g. "1.0.4-rc1")
  82:                 # so int conversion doesn't ValueError. The numeric
  83:                 # prefix is enough for ordering.
  84:                 break
  85:         return tuple(parts) if parts else (0,)
  86:     except (ValueError, AttributeError):
  87:         return (0,)
  88: 
  89: 
  90: def resolve_openai_codex_plugin_root() -> Path:
  91:     """Return Path to the active openai-codex plugin directory.
  92: 
  93:     See module docstring for resolution order. Always returns a Path —
  94:     callers downstream (e.g. `_PLUGIN_ROOT / 'schemas/review-output.schema.json'`)
  95:     will encounter their own existence checks; this helper does NOT
  96:     verify the path's contents, only that the directory itself resolves.
  97: 
  98:     Smoke-tested cycle-590 ship 8 against a 1-version cache (only 1.0.4
  99:     installed); int-tuple sort verified manually against synthetic
 100:     `(1.0.4, 1.0.10, 1.1.0, 2.0.0)` expected order = `[2.0.0, 1.1.0, 1.0.10, 1.0.4]`.
 101:     """
 102:     override = os.environ.get(_ENV_OVERRIDE, "").strip()
 103:     if override:
 104:         p = Path(override)
 105:         if p.is_dir():
 106:             return p
 107:         # Env override points to non-existent path → fall through to
 108:         # cache-glob lookup. Could log a warning but stdlib-only fail-open
 109:         # per hooks/CLAUDE.md sys.exit conventions.
 110: 
 111:     if _BASE_PATH.is_dir():
 112:         try:
 113:             version_dirs = sorted(
 114:                 (p for p in _BASE_PATH.iterdir() if p.is_dir()),
 115:                 key=_version_key,
 116:                 reverse=True,
 117:             )
 118:             if version_dirs:
 119:                 return version_dirs[0]
 120:         except OSError:
 121:             pass
 122: 
 123:     return _BASE_PATH / _FALLBACK_VERSION
 124: 
 125: 
 126: __all__ = ["resolve_openai_codex_plugin_root"]
